# Mistakes found in published sources

The atlas's workers read papers and books line by line to extract and plan their mathematics, and they record every mistake they find in them: misprints (slips whose intended meaning is clear), errors (false statements or steps that fail) and gaps (steps asserted without an adequate proof). A finding counts as confirmed once an independent reviewer has checked it at its locator. Each finding says where its worker looked for an existing correction; a new one is not corrected in any erratum or later version they found. Nothing here has been sent to the authors.

Collected by `scripts/errata.py` from the paper extractions and blueprint packets (research/blueprint/PROTOCOL.md, section 18); the data is in `data/source-issues.json`.

0 new mistakes confirmed · 426 awaiting review · 58 already corrected in print · 0 rejected on review · 8 extractions and packets not yet checked.

## New mistakes, confirmed

None yet.

## New mistakes awaiting review

### Bakker–Klingler–Tsimerman, "Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci", Journal of the American Mathematical Society (2020) (`PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20`)

- **Error** at Theorem 4.1, p.13 of the author copy (= arXiv 1810.04801v2, p.13); the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Theorem 4.1. Let V be a polarized variation of pure Hodge structures of weight k over the punctured polydisk (∆*)^n, with period map Φ: (∆*)^n → S_{Γ,G,M}. Then Φ is R_an,exp-definable." (∆ is the unit disk, p.4.)`; it should be `Definability holds only after shrinking. For some radius 0 < ρ < 1, the restriction of Φ to (∆*_ρ)^n is R_an,exp-definable, and then for every ρ < 1 by compactness of the remaining annular pieces. The reduction in §4.1 must use charts whose shrunken polydisks still cover the compactification.`. Counterexample (n = 1, trivial monodromy). Let f(z) = exp(−(1+z)/(1−z)) on the unit disk, so |f| < 1, and τ = 2i + εf with ε small, so that τ(∆) lies in a disk on which π: H → Γ\H is injective (Γ torsion-free, e.g. Γ(3)). τ is the period map of a polarized weight-one integral VHS. But Φ^{−1}(π(2i + ε/e)) contains the points z_n = 2πin/(2 + 2πin), n ≠ 0, where f = 1/e. These form an infinite discrete set accumulating at 1, which is impossible for a fibre of a definable map. The proof itself allows for this: it says 'after maybe shrinking the polydisk' (p.14), and its Siegel set S_H (y > 1) covers only |q| < e^{−2π}. Theorem 1.3 is unaffected, since §4.1 can use finitely many shrunken charts. Checked numerically: |f(z_n) − 1/e| < 1e−8 for 0 < |n| ≤ 2000. Noted by the extraction (item local-outer-boundary-obstruction). Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).
- **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 4.5, p.15 of the author copy (= arXiv v2 p.15); the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1) (it affects the proof). The source says `"Consider for each positive integer m and real number c the function f_{m,c} which we obtain from f by setting z_1 = mz_2 + c. Note that the assumptions of the lemma still apply to f_{m,c}, and it follows by induction on n that f_{m,c} ≪ 1 on all of Σ_{n−1}"`; it should be `State the lemma, and verify its curve hypothesis, on the wider sectors Σ_n^R = {|x_i| < R, y_1 ≥ ⋯ ≥ y_n > 1} for every R, with constants depending on R. The substitution then maps Σ_{n−1}^{R′} into Σ_n^{mR′+c}, and the induction goes through unchanged. In the application in §4.5, the curve bounds come from Schmid's one-variable Corollary 5.29 and the norm estimates of Theorem 4.8, which hold for real parts in any bounded interval; Theorem 1.5 is already stated on H^n_{R,η} for all R.`. Σ_n is defined with 0 < x_i < 1 (Definition 4.4). The proof uses c = 0, 1, …, r_1 and m ≥ 1. For c ≥ 1, x_1 = mx_2 + c > 1, so Σ_n ∩ {z_1 = mz_2 + c} is empty. For c = 0 it is only the strip x_2 < 1/m. So the hypothesis gives no information about f_{m,c} on Σ_{n−1}. The functions extend there (the coefficients in O are periodic in x and f is polynomial in x), but the bound is assumed only on Σ_n. With the R-uniform hypothesis, the finite difference in c isolates the top x_1-degree, the Vandermonde step in m isolates each y-power, and |x_1| < 1 on Σ_n finishes the proof, as printed. Noted by the extraction (verification gap G4); the repair is given by this continuation. Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).
- **Error** at Proof of Theorem 1.5 (§4.5), p.18 of the author copy; the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1). The source says `"Moreover, if b is (e′, C′)-reduced and e is a basis for which condition (3) of Definition 4.11 holds for some C > 0, then b will also be (e, C″)-reduced for some C″ = C″(e, C, e′, C′) > 0."`; it should be `Condition (2), the ordering b(e_i) < C b(e_j) for i < j, transfers only up to a permutation of e. Conclude that b is (σe, C″)-reduced for some permutation σ, which is one of finitely many.`. Take e′ = (e_1, e_2), e = (e_2, e_1) and b = diag(1, T) with T ≥ 1. Then b is (e′, 2)-reduced and satisfies condition (3) for e (b(e_2)b(e_1) = T = det b), but condition (2) for e would need T = b(e_2) < C″b(e_1) = C″ for all T. The proof immediately adds 'we may assume (2) as there are only finitely many orderings of the basis', which is the corrected form, so Theorem 1.5 is unaffected. Noted by the extraction (item basis-change-reducedness). Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).
- **Misprint** at Appendix A.1, p.20 of the author copy; the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1). The source says `"Given X a real-analytic n-manifold with corners, one defines its boundary ∂X (cf. [Joy12, def. 2.6]. This is a real-analytic n-manifold with corners for n > 0"`; it should be `∂X is a real-analytic (n − 1)-manifold with corners.`. The boundary of an n-manifold with corners in Joyce's sense is (n − 1)-dimensional; for [0,∞) it is a point. The appendix itself later says 'the compact real-analytic (n − 1)-manifold with corners ∂X' (proof of Proposition A.2). Noted by the extraction (item corner-boundary). Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).

### Bakker–Tsimerman, "p-torsion monodromy representations of elliptic curves over geometric function fields", Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), no. 3 (`PAPER-BAKKER-TSIMERMAN-16`)

- **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 23, p.735, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 709–744 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 814976ee…f40a). The source says `"dd^c(f∘χ) = −(1/2π) dd^c log(|1 − z w̄|² − |z − w|²) + dd^c log|z − w|² = −(1/2π) dd^c log[(1 − |z|²)(1 − |w|²)] + [Δ_D] = (1/4π) ω_{D×D} + [Δ_D]."`; it should be `The coefficient in the first two lines is −1: f∘χ = log|z − w|² − log(|1 − z w̄|² − |z − w|²), so dd^c(f∘χ) = −dd^c log[(1 − |z|²)(1 − |w|²)] + [Δ_D] = (1/4π) ω_{D×D} + [Δ_D].`. With f(s) = log(s/(1 − s)) and χ = |z − w|²/|1 − z w̄|², f∘χ is the stated difference of logarithms with coefficient 1; the final line is consistent with the paper's d^c normalization only with coefficient −1 in front of the first term. Found by the review REV-PAPER-BAKKER-TSIMERMAN-16 (item 117). Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-TSIMERMAN-16/E10`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2016/184-3/p02, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked there; B. Bakker's homepage https://benjamin-bakker.github.io/ links the authors' erratum (pdf) under this paper; it was read in full; arXiv 1403.7168: v1 (27 March 2014) and v2 (3 May 2016), both before the erratum; no later version; web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: only the authors' erratum was found.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 29, p.742, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 709–744 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 814976ee…f40a). The source says `"Thus, Ram(α) = o(Deg C), and Riemann–Hurwitz applied to α yields 2g(C) − 2 = o(Deg C)."`; it should be `Riemann–Hurwitz for α : C → P¹ gives 2g(C) − 2 = −2 deg α + Ram(α) ≤ o(Deg C). The inequality is what the contradiction with 2g(C) − 2 ≫ Deg(C) needs.`. The printed equality drops the term −2 deg α, which is not o(Deg C) in general. Found by the extraction (item 90). Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-TSIMERMAN-16/E11`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2016/184-3/p02, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked there; B. - **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 4.1, p.3, in the authors' erratum (B. Bakker and J. Tsimerman, Erratum, 5 pp., https://benjamin-bakker.github.io/P.torsion.erratum.pdf, SHA-256 db8effa1…acc1). The source says `"mult_{CM−∩Δ̄}(T*_m C) ≪ … ≪ vol(T*_m C ∩ B(Δ̄, ε)) ≪ (sinh(ρ_{X(p)}/2)/sinh(ε)) vol(T*_m C)"`; it should be `The factor is sinh(ε)/sinh(ρ_{X(p)}/2): Proposition 3.1 gives vol(C ∩ B(Δ̄,ε)) ≤ (sinh ε/sinh R) vol(C ∩ B(Δ̄,R)) for 0 < ε < R < ρ/2, and letting R ↑ ρ_{X(p)}/2 gives the small factor.`. Proposition 3.1 says (1/sinh r)·vol(C ∩ B(Δ̄,r)) increases with r, so the small ball has at most the proportion sinh ε/sinh R of the large one. The printed reciprocal is a large factor (ρ_{X(p)} ~ 2 log p) and would not give the p^{−1} saving the next line states, (p^{−2δ₂} + d₃p^{−1}) Deg(C). - **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 15, pp.726–727, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 709–744 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 814976ee…f40a). The source says `"Since M′² = −1, it follows that M′ is either equal to t̃ or −t̃" … "Thus g = a + b(0 1; −1 0), … and (x,y) lies on T_m where m = det g = a² + b²."`; it should be `Treat the order-three case separately: there t̃ satisfies t̃² − t̃ + 1 = 0 (H ∩ M₂(Z) ≅ Z[e^{2πi/3}]), so M′ satisfies the same Eisenstein relation and M′ ∈ {t̃, 1 − t̃}, and g = a + b·t₀ with m = det g = a² + ab + b². The erratum does not revisit this. Found by the extraction (item 46). - **Error** at §2.5, Hecke operators, p.717, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 709–744 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 814976ee…f40a). The source says `T_n is defined (p.716) as the closure of pairs related by a cyclic isogeny of degree n, and then: "The two projection maps α, β : T_n → X(p) both have degree deg(T_n) = σ₁(n) = Σ_{d|n} d".`; it should be `For the cyclic correspondence deg(T_n) = ψ(n) = n∏_{ℓ|n}(1 + 1/ℓ); σ₁(n) is the degree of the full correspondence over all subgroups of order n.`. For n = 4 there are six cyclic subgroups of order 4 but σ₁(4) = 7 (the extra subgroup is E[2]). The later estimates use only a polynomial bound such as deg(T_m) = O(m²), which both satisfy. Found by the extraction (item 25). ### Bary-Soroker–Koukoulopoulos–Kozma, "Irreducibility of random polynomials: general measures", Inventiones Mathematicae (2023) (`PAPER-BARYSOROKER-KOUKOULOPOULOS-KOZMA-23`)

- **Misprint** at arXiv:2007.14567v3, Theorem7 p13 and its proof p14 (it affects a stated result). The source says `P(A has no divisors of degree≤θn | a₀≠0)≤C₁n^(−c)`; it should be `Bound the event that a nonconstant divisor of degree≤θn exists; equivalently bound the complement below by1−C₁n^(−c). In the proof's last paragraph insert the missing negation as well.`. The preceding propositions bound presence of small factors, and the last deduction is irreducibility from absence of factors through n/2. Units must be excluded throughout. - **Error** at arXiv:2007.14567v3, Remark3.2, p23 (it affects a stated result). The source says `μhat(a/Q)=H^(−1)Σ_{k≤H}e(af(k)/Q); #{x modQ:f(x)=b}≤d^ω(Q)`; it should be `For the Fourier identity use the pushforward of the uniform input law, or require f injective on that input set. Uniform image-set measure differs from pushforward when values collide: f(x)=x(x−3), x=1,2,3 gives values−2,−2,0 with different masses. Also f=5x mod5 has5 roots at b=0, exceeding degree1. Item122 makes both hypotheses explicit. ### Bennett–Siksek, "A conjecture of Erdős, supersingular primes and short character sums", Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), no. 2 (`PAPER-BENNETT-SIKSEK-20`)

- **Error** at Lemma 3.2, p.361, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 355–392 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3920a752…). The source says `"Its discriminant is Δ_a = 64(a_a b_a c_a)² = (2⁸/g⁶)(n+id)²(n+jd)²(n+(2j−i)d)²."`; it should be `Δ_a = 16(a_a b_a c_a)² = (2⁶/g⁶)(n+id)²(n+jd)²(n+(2j−i)d)².`. For y² = x³ + a₂x² + a₄x, Δ = 16a₄²(a₂² − 4a₄). For E_a: Y² = X(X−a)(X+c), a₂ = c − a and a₄ = −ac, so Δ = 16a²c²(a+c)² = 16(abc)² since a+b+c = 0; with b_a = −2(n+jd)/g this is 2⁶/g⁶ times the product. The extraction found this (item 16, report D1); the review re-derived it. Recorded as `PAPER-BENNETT-SIKSEK-20/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2020/191-2/p02, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or corrigendum is linked; arXiv 1709.01022: only v1 (4 September 2017) exists, which predates the published text; no later version; web search for an erratum, corrigendum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found; S. Siksek's homepage (samirsiksek.github.io) lists the paper with no correction; M. A. Bennett's homepage and CV page (personal.math.ubc.ca/~bennett) list no correction.
- **Error** at Theorem 5, equation (26), p.374, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 355–392 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3920a752…). The source says `"Let χ be a primitive Dirichlet character of conductor N. Then Σ_{m≤X} χ(m)Λ(m) = δ_χX − X^{β_χ}/β_χ + O(X exp(−c log X/(√(log X) + log N))·(log N)⁴)."`; it should be `State it for N > 1, or replace (log N)⁴ by (log 2N)⁴; for the principal character (N = 1) the printed error term is 0.`. With N = 1 the formula would assert ψ(X) = X exactly. The paper uses only nonprincipal characters with N > 1. The extraction found this (item 89, report D13). The source says `"Let π_i be characters of conductor q_i for 1 ≤ i ≤ r", applied with "ψ … principal of conductor M₂" and a factorization with "(d) 1 < q_r ≤ k^{7/16}".`; it should be `The later factors are characters of modulus q_i (a principal character has conductor 1). The source says `"Checking that in (36) we have K₀(10^{−5}) < exp(exp(10⁶)), we may thus apply Theorem 8 (Roth's theorem)", with (36) K₀(δ) = exp(exp(132 log(2)·δ^{−1})) and hypothesis (37) k ≥ exp(exp(10⁶)).`; it should be `K₀(10^{−5}) = exp(exp(132 log 2·10⁵)) ≈ exp(exp(9.15·10⁶)) > exp(exp(10⁶)). The argument works with the threshold k ≥ exp(exp(10⁷)); every other estimate of the proof holds there.`. 132·log 2·10⁵ ≈ 9.1495·10⁶ > 10⁶, so the claimed comparison fails and Proposition 9.1 is not proved at the stated threshold (it may still be true). Theorem 2 has an unspecified effective k₀, so it is unaffected. Checked on the page image of p.381. The extraction found this (items 47, 108 and 149, report D16). ### Benoist–Wittenberg, "On the integral Hodge conjecture for real varieties, I", Inventiones Mathematicae (2020) (`PAPER-BENOIST-WITTENBERG-20`)

- **Error** at Theorem 3.16(ii), p.55, and Theorem A(ii), p.4, in the published version, Inventiones mathematicae 222 (2020), 1–77 (author-hosted copy with journal pagination, SHA-256 daeb43ec…46a9) (it affects a stated result). The source says `Theorem A: "Let X be a smooth, proper and geometrically irreducible real variety, of dimension d ≥ 1. Assume that X satisfies the real integral Hodge conjecture for 1-cycles and that H²(X, O_X) = 0. … (ii) There exists a geometrically irreducible curve of even geometric genus in X if and only if the natural map Pic(X)[2^∞] → Pic(X_C)^G[2^∞] is onto." Theorem 3.16 assumes "dim(X) > 0".`; it should be `Add the hypothesis d ≥ 2 (or X(R) = ∅); the proof, which goes through Proposition 3.9, needs d ≥ 2 when X(R) ≠ ∅.`. Counterexample in dimension one: X = E, the real elliptic curve y² = x³ − x. Here H²(E, O_E) = 0 and the Hodge conjecture for 1-cycles (codimension 0) holds. The only geometrically irreducible curve in E is E itself, of geometric genus 1, so the left side fails. Since E(R) ≠ ∅, Br(R) → Br(E) is injective and Pic(E) → Pic(E_C)^G is an isomorphism, so the right side holds. Proposition 3.9 (a curve of odd geometric genus exists) is stated for dimension ≥ 2, as the introduction says (p.4). The surface and higher-dimensional cases, which are the paper's applications, are unaffected. Found by the extraction (items printed-even-genus, main-even-genus). Recorded as `PAPER-BENOIST-WITTENBERG-20/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: O. Wittenberg's publications page https://www.math.univ-paris13.fr/~wittenberg/ lists this paper and its sequel (part II, J. Éc. polytech. Math. 7 (2020)) with no erratum for part I, while it does list an erratum for another Benoist–Wittenberg paper (checked 22 September 2026); arXiv 1801.00872: v1 (2018), v2 (2019), v3 (11 March 2020); v3 has the same Theorem 3.16; web search for an erratum or corrigendum to part I, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Misprint** at §2.2, paragraph before Example 2.5, p.37, in the published version, Inventiones mathematicae 222 (2020), 1–77 (author-hosted copy with journal pagination, SHA-256 daeb43ec…46a9). The source says `"Namely, let X be a smooth and proper variety over a real closed field R and let k ≥ 0 be even, so that Z(2k) = Z(k). We can view ω^{2k} … as an element of H^{2k}_G(X(C), Z(k)). This class is Hodge if R = R, being torsion, and it belongs to H^{2k}_G(X(C), Z(k))_0 if (and only if) X(R) = ∅."`; it should be `"let k > 0 be even".`. For k = 0, ω^0 = 1 is not torsion, and it is the class of X itself, hence algebraic and in the subgroup cut out by the topological condition even when X(R) ≠ ∅; the "if and only if" fails. For even k > 0 the statement is as printed. Noted by the extraction (item constant-omega). Recorded as `PAPER-BENOIST-WITTENBERG-20/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: O. Wittenberg's publications page https://www.math.univ-paris13.fr/~wittenberg/ lists this paper and its sequel (part II, J. Éc. polytech. Math. 7 (2020)) with no erratum for part I, while it does list an erratum for another Benoist–Wittenberg paper (checked 22 September 2026); arXiv 1801.00872: v1 (2018), v2 (2019), v3 (11 March 2020); v3 has the same Theorem 3.16; web search for an erratum or corrigendum to part I, 22 September 2026: none found.

### Bhargava–Shankar–Taniguchi–Thorne–Tsimerman–Zhao, "Bounds on 2-torsion in class groups of number fields and integral points on elliptic curves", Journal of the American Mathematical Society (2020) (`PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-TANIGUCHI-ETAL-20`)

- **Misprint** at Bombieri–Pila, The number of integral points on arcs and ovals, Oxford author preprint, Lemma 7 proof, printed p.14 (PDF index 13). (it affects the proof). The source says `lambda = ell A^(-1/k)/N`; it should be `Use lambda = ell A^(1/k)/N, where ell is the interval length. Then Taylor gives lambda^k≤lambda^(k−1)+...+lambda+2.`. Divide the Taylor inequality by N. The k-th-order term is ell^k A/N^k, the k-th power of the corrected dimensionless quantity. The polynomial identity in supplement BP2 proves lambda≤2, retaining the stated interval bound. Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-TANIGUCHI-ETAL-20/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Web search on 2026-09-22: Bombieri Pila "arcs and ovals" erratum; Web search on 2026-09-22: "Bombieri" "Pila" "Since p"; Read the Oxford-hosted author preprint at https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/pila/Ovals.pdf, especially printed pp.14–17; no existing correction was located in these searches..
- **Misprint** at Bombieri–Pila Oxford author preprint, Theorem 4 proof, printed p.17 (PDF index 16), just before the estimate for H+K. (it affects the proof). The source says `Since p ≤ q`; it should be `Since q ≤ p.`. For the selected monomials X^i Y^j, q=Σj while p=Σ(i+j), and i≥0. Since A>1 this gives A^q≤A^p, the direction needed in the next estimate. Supplement BP4 writes out the entire subsequent bound. Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-TANIGUCHI-ETAL-20/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: Web search on 2026-09-22: Bombieri Pila "arcs and ovals" erratum; Web search on 2026-09-22: "Bombieri" "Pila" "Since p"; Read the Oxford-hosted author preprint at https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/pila/Ovals.pdf, especially printed pp.14–17; no existing correction was located in these searches..
- **Gap** at Bombieri–Pila Oxford author preprint, Theorem 4 proof, printed p.17 (PDF index 16), final choice of the monomial cutoff δ. (it affects the proof). The source says `δ = 2 sqrt(log N/(d log log N))`; it should be `Take the floor of the displayed real number and prove the threshold and error inequalities; supplement BP5 proves δ≥2d and 4 log N/δ+9dδ log δ≤11 sqrt(d log N log log N).`. The monomial cutoff used to define D and p must be an integer. The printed real choice alone does not supply an admissible cutoff or justify the exact constant. Monotonicity of the objective between its floor and that real choice proves that rounding down preserves the estimate; the stated theorem and threshold need not change. - **Misprint** at BSTTTZ, Taniguchi author copy dated 18 February 2017, proof of Theorem 2.1, printed p.4 (PDF index 3), product formula for the volume of B. (it affects the proof). The source says `sqrt(pi) |Disc(K)|^(1/(2n)) |alpha|_v^(-1/m)`; it should be `For every complex place, square the whole displayed factor: its disc contributes pi R_v^2, not sqrt(pi) R_v. The body volume is 2^r pi^s sqrt(D_K)/N(I).`. A complex coordinate is two real dimensions. With K=Q(i), I=O_K and alpha=1, the radius is sqrt(2); the disc area is 2pi rather than sqrt(2pi). The latter is below the Minkowski threshold 4 in the ordinary metric, whereas the correct area is above it. The existing balanced-body item already uses the corrected formula, so the theorem and its route are unchanged. Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-TANIGUCHI-ETAL-20/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: Web search on 2026-09-22: "Bounds on 2-torsion in class groups" corrigendum; Read and visually inspected https://www.math.kobe-u.ac.jp/HOME/tani/bstttz.pdf, printed p.4. No existing correction located in the search.; AMS final article/PDF paths and the author publication page were attempted but did not yield the final revision; this record makes no claim about its typography..

### Bhargava–Shankar–Wang, "Squarefree values of polynomial discriminants I", Inventiones Mathematicae (2022) (`PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-WANG-22`)

- **Error** at §1, p.4 (arXiv v3). The source says `"Then p² strongly divides ∆(f) if and only if f modulo p has at least two distinct multiple roots in F̄_p, or has a root in F_p of multiplicity at least 3; and p² weakly divides ∆(f) if p² | ∆(f) but f modulo p has only one multiple root in F_p and this root is a simple double root."`; it should be `This holds for odd p. For p = 2, 4 always divides Δ(f) strongly when 2 | Δ(f), so W^{(2)}_m is empty for even m.`. By Stickelberger's congruence, the discriminant of a monic integer polynomial is ≡ 0 or 1 mod 4, so 2 | Δ(f + 2g) implies 4 | Δ(f + 2g) for every g. For example f = x² + 1 ≡ (x + 1)² mod 2 has a single simple double root, yet Δ(x² + a_1x + a_2) = a_1² − 4a_2 ≡ 0 mod 4 whenever a_1 is even. A brute-force check over all f mod p confirms the criterion for p = 3 (n ≤ 5) and p = 5 (n ≤ 4), and finds failures at p = 2 for every n = 2, …, 5. The proofs only use the implication "two multiple roots or a triple root ⇒ strong", which holds at p = 2 as well (in Proposition 2.2), and the tail estimates concern large primes, so nothing else changes. Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-WANG-22/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Invent. ### Bhatt et al., "Globally +-regular varieties and the minimal model program for threefolds in mixed characteristic", Publications Mathématiques de l'IHÉS (2023) (`PAPER-BHATT-ETAL-23`)

- **Error** at Lemma 2.56, second assertion, p.101 of the published version; the same wording is arXiv v1 Lemma 2.37 (p.20), v2 Lemma 2.50 (p.26) and v3 Lemma 2.56 (p.29) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"More generally, let D be a fixed divisor and let A be a semiample Q-Cartier Q-divisor such that A ∼_Q M + Λ, where M is an effective Cartier divisor with no exceptional curve of Y → X in its support, and −δD ≤ Λ ≤ δD for δ > 0. Take x ∈ F ∩ M of multiplicity k ∈ Z_{>0} in M. Then ε_sa(A; x) converges to k when δ → 0."`; it should be `Only a lower bound holds. For every rational γ with 0 < γ < k there is δ₀ > 0, depending only on f, D and γ, such that ε_sa(A; x) = ε(A; x) ≥ k − γ for all δ < δ₀ and all such A, M, Λ and x. Equivalently, liminf_{δ→0} ε_sa(A; x) ≥ k, uniformly. This is exactly what the printed proof establishes; there is no upper bound.`. Counterexample with Λ = 0. Let k be a field of characteristic p > 0, X = A²_k, and f: Y → X the blow-up of the origin with exceptional curve E = F. Let M be the sum of the strict transforms of the two axes. Then M ∼ −2E, so M is effective, Cartier, globally generated over X (O_Y(−E) = m·O_Y) and has no exceptional component. Let x = E ∩ (strict transform of the y-axis), so mult_x M = 1 = k. Blow up x, with exceptional curve G and strict transform E′. Then E′² = −2, G² = −1, E′·G = 1 and π*M − tG ∼ −2E′ − (2+t)G, which has degree 2 − t on E′ and t on G. Hence ε(M; x) = 2. At t = 2 the ideal (x, y²) pulls back to O(−E′ − 2G) (charts x = ab, y = b, then a = c, b = cd, giving c²d, and a = ef, b = f, giving f²). So π*M − 2G ∼ −2E′ − 4G is globally generated and ε_sa(M; x) = 2. Taking A = M and Λ = 0 (any D), ε_sa(A; x) = 2 for every δ, not converging to k = 1. The proof only shows that π*A − (k − γ)E is nef. The consumers need only the lower bound: Proposition 8.15 (p.173) uses the first assertion ε_sa(M; x) ≥ 1, which is correct, and Claim 8.21 (p.178) needs ε_sa > 2 for k = 3. So the paper's results are unaffected. Checked on the page image and by exact arithmetic. Noted by the extraction (diagnostic A, gap-seshadri-limit); verified and proved in the corrected form by this continuation. - **Misprint** at Proposition 6.28, p.154 of the published version (and arXiv v3 Proposition 6.28, p.71). The source says `"Let Δ ≥ 0 be a Q-divisor on X such that (X, Δ) is globally +-regular (completely purely globally +-regular over R, resp.). Suppose that Δ_Y ≥ 0, where K_Y + Δ_Y = f*(K_X + Δ). Then (Y, Δ_Y) is globally +-regular (globally +-regular, resp.)."`; it should be `The conclusion in the resp. case is "completely purely globally +-regular over R", with the pure boundary S_Y the strict transform of S and B_Y = Δ_Y − S_Y. This is what arXiv v2 (Proposition 6.28, p.64) prints, and arXiv v1 (Proposition 6.22, p.48) prints "purely ... purely".`. The printed resp. conclusion is false. Take R = k[[t]] with k of characteristic p, X = Y = Spec R, f = id and Δ = S = V(t). Then (X, S) is purely globally +-regular. For a finite normal R′ ⊇ R with uniformizer s and ramification e, the map R → s^{1−e}R′ splits because R′ is R-free and 1 ∉ t·s^{1−e}R′ = sR′. But (X, S) is not globally +-regular: already for the identity cover, a splitting of O_X → O_X(S) = t^{−1}R would send 1 = t·t^{−1} into tR. The cited proof (Proposition 6.26, which applies because R is complete, together with Lemma 4.27 with B′ = B_Y) proves the pure conclusion. The consumer in Theorem 7.14 (p.165) uses the pure conclusion. In arXiv v3 the qualifier is visibly garbled as "( ly globally +-regular, resp.)", so the published wording is a typesetting slip. Checked on the page images of v3 p.71 and published p.154. Noted by the extraction (diagnostic B, gap-pure-birational); the version history was established by this continuation. ### Binyamini, "Point counting for foliations over number fields", Forum of Mathematics, Pi (2022) (`PAPER-BINYAMINI-22`)

- **Misprint** at Lemma 15 and proof, (34)–(35), p.11 (published version). The source says `"S(X ∩ B², ε) ≤ (2^m/c(m)) vol(X)·ε^{−2m}" … "vol(X ∩ B_p) ≥ c(n)(ε/2)^{2m}"`; it should be `S(X ∩ B², ε) ≤ (2^{2m}/c(m)) vol(X) ε^{−2m}, from vol(X ∩ B_p) ≥ c(m)(ε/2)^{2m}.`. X has dimension m, so the Lelong bound involves c(m), the volume of the unit ball of ℂ^m. Dividing vol(X) by c(m)(ε/2)^{2m} gives the factor 2^{2m}. Only O_n-constants change. Recorded as `PAPER-BINYAMINI-22/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2009.00892 (v1, 2 September 2020, the only version); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 10 (2022) e6 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2021.20: no update or correction relation.

The source says `"by a dimension-counting argument using Lemma 19, the codimension of π_Φ(Σ_Ṽ) is positive" … "Set Z = Clo π(Σ_{W̃_{k+1}}) … Z has positive codimension"`; it should be `Restrict to the part of Σ_Ṽ lying over M ∖ Σ_V (resp. over the p with dim(V ∩ L_p) < n − k): Z := Clo π_Φ(Σ_Ṽ ∖ π_M^{-1}(Σ_V)).`. Over points p ∈ Σ_V every Φ lies in Σ_Ṽ, so π_Φ(Σ_Ṽ) is all of M_D whenever Σ_V ≠ ∅. Lemma 19 and the target condition (44) concern only p ∉ Σ_V. Recorded as `PAPER-BINYAMINI-22/E7`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2009.00892 (v1, 2 September 2020, the only version); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 10 (2022) e6 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2021.20: no update or correction relation.
- **Error** at Proposition 51 and Theorem 9, Appendix A, pp.36–37 (published version) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Proposition 51. Suppose L is defined over a number field. Then inf_{φ ∈ Aut(ℙ¹)} ‖a₀(φ*L)‖ = e^{−poly_n(δ_L)}. Proof. … Now the operator L′ := L + 1 is also Fuchsian …"`; it should be `Assume in addition that L + 1 is Fuchsian. This holds when every finite zero of a₀ has order ≤ n and deg a₀ ≥ n, and in particular for the Legendre Picard–Fuchs operator (87), a₀ = λ(1−λ), n = 2, which is the only operator to which the paper applies Theorem 9. Under this normalization the proof of Proposition 51 is correct, and Theorem 9 follows as written.`. For a general Fuchsian operator, L + 1 need not be Fuchsian, and both statements fail. Take L = ∂_t, which is Fuchsian: its only singular point ∞ is regular singular (∂_t = −z(z∂_z), z = 1/t). Then φ(s) = κs gives φ*L = κ^{-1}∂_s and inf_φ ‖a₀(φ*L)‖ = 0. In Theorem 9 take b(t) = (t − t₀)/(2r) (|b| ≤ 1 on D^{1/2}) and f = (t − t₀)²/(4r), so j_z f(t₀) = 0 while |∂_z f(t₀ + r/2)| = r/4, unbounded in r. Here L + 1 = ∂ + 1 has an irregular singularity at ∞. Theorems 1–8 are unaffected. ### Bresciani, "On the birational section conjecture with strong birationality assumptions", Inventiones Mathematicae (2024) (`PAPER-BRESCIANI-24`)

- **Misprint** at §2, two citations of [Bre21b, Proposition A.18], v3 p.5 (published pp.133 and 135), in arXiv 2108.13397v3 (15 September 2023, the final author version; PDF SHA-256 e23989fb…b722); published page numbers are those the extraction recorded from the open-access publisher PDF (SHA-256 77c20bc7…e148), which could not be re-fetched here. The source says `"Furthermore, the étale fundamental gerbe behaves well with respect to base change in characteristic 0 [Bre21b, Proposition A.18]." and "If k′/k is a field extension and c : Spec k′ → C is a point, the induced morphism Π_{X_c/k′} → Π_{X/C} ×_C Spec k′ is an isomorphism: using the fact that the étale fundamental gerbe behaves well under base change [Bre21b, Proposition A.18] …"`; it should be `[Bre21b, Proposition A.23].`. In the published [Bre21b] (Algebr. Geom. 8 (2021), 231–267, the version cited), Proposition A.18 (p.262) treats only algebraic separable extensions k′/k. Proposition A.23 (p.264) gives Π_{X_{k′}/k′} ≅ Π_{X/k} ×_k k′ for every field extension of a characteristic-0 field and geometrically connected concentrated X. The paper needs arbitrary extensions, above all k(t)/k and residue fields of points of C, so A.23 is the result meant; A.23's own proof starts from A.18. Both statements were read in the published [Bre21b] (SHA-256 768ca87a…2908). Noted by the extraction (item 26). Recorded as `PAPER-BRESCIANI-24/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Inventiones mathematicae: Crossref record of DOI 10.1007/s00222-023-01220-6 checked for updates, 22 September 2026: none registered; arXiv 2108.13397: v1 (30 August 2021), v2 (12 February 2022), v3 (15 September 2023, the latest); web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found; F. Pop, "Generalizations and minimalistic refinements of the t-birational Section Conjecture", arXiv 2604.26131, was searched for comments on this paper and makes none.
- **Error** at §4, the paragraph after Corollary 15 on parabolic curves, v3 p.11 (published p.141), in arXiv 2108.13397v3 (15 September 2023, the final author version; PDF SHA-256 e23989fb…b722); published page numbers are those the extraction recorded from the open-access publisher PDF (SHA-256 77c20bc7…e148), which could not be re-fetched here (it affects the proof). The source says `"We say that a curve X is parabolic if it has genus 0 and the degree of X̄ ∖ X is at most 2. If X is proper of genus 0, then there is a unique Galois section, and it is geometric if and only if X = P¹. If X is parabolic affine, every Galois section of X is cuspidal (the only non-trivial case is settled by [Sch15, Theorem A]). Because of this, we can always assume that X is non-parabolic."`; it should be `The sentence is false as stated. What the later reductions need is a statement about the t-birationally liftable (or birationally liftable) sections of a parabolic affine X, which have to be treated directly, including the case where X̄ ∖ X is a single point of degree 2. The reductions to non-parabolic X rest on this: "We may assume that X is non-parabolic" in the proof of Lemma 16, and "it is immediate to check that t-b.l. sections are quasi-t-b.l." after Definition 18, where the identity map is not available for parabolic X.`. Counterexample. Let k = Q and X = P¹_Q ∖ V(T² − 2). Then X is affine, of genus 0, with boundary a single point of degree 2, so it is parabolic. The paper defines a Galois section to be cuspidal if it is associated with a k-rational point of X̄ ∖ X (§1). The boundary has no Q-rational point, so X has no cuspidal sections. But 0 ∈ X(Q), so X has a geometric section. Schmidt's theorem concerns the existence of rational points and splitting; it does not make a degree-2 boundary point rational. The paper's Theorems A–C are not claimed to fail here, only this justification of the reduction. Noted by the extraction (item 77, with the reduction gap in items 81, 96 and 112). Recorded as `PAPER-BRESCIANI-24/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: Inventiones mathematicae: Crossref record of DOI 10.1007/s00222-023-01220-6 checked for updates, 22 September 2026: none registered; arXiv 2108.13397: v1 (30 August 2021), v2 (12 February 2022), v3 (15 September 2023, the latest); web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found; F. Pop, "Generalizations and minimalistic refinements of the t-birational Section Conjecture", arXiv 2604.26131, was searched for comments on this paper and makes none.
- **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 26, v3 p.17 (published p.148), in arXiv 2108.13397v3 (15 September 2023, the final author version; PDF SHA-256 e23989fb…b722); published page numbers are those the extraction recorded from the open-access publisher PDF (SHA-256 77c20bc7…e148), which could not be re-fetched here (it affects the proof). The source says `"For every open subset U ⊂ X, denote by ι_U(s) the image of s in S_{U/k}, by hypothesis it is cuspidal. By [Sti12, Theorem 17 (2)], there exists a unique rational point x ∈ X̄(k) associated with ι_X(s) … For every open subset U ⊂ X, denote by P_{U,x} ⊂ S_{U/k} the packet of cuspidal sections associated with x. … Since ι_U(s) ∈ P_{U,x} for every U ⊂ X and s is the limit of the sections ι_U(s), we get that s is cuspidal associated with x too."`; it should be `By hypothesis each ι_U(s) is geometric or cuspidal, not necessarily cuspidal. Once the associated point x ∈ X̄(k) is found, restrict to the cofinal family of opens U with x ∉ U. For those, ι_U(s) is cuspidal at x, and S_{k(X)/k} = lim_U S_{U/k} may be computed along this family, so the conclusion follows.`. The hypothesis of the lemma is that every birationally liftable section is geometric or cuspidal. If x ∈ X, then for opens U containing x the section ι_U(s) is the geometric section at x, which is not in the cuspidal packet P_{U,x}. So "ι_U(s) ∈ P_{U,x} for every U ⊂ X" fails for those U, although the limit argument goes through on the cofinal subfamily. Noted by the extraction (item 127). Recorded as `PAPER-BRESCIANI-24/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: Inventiones mathematicae: Crossref record of DOI 10.1007/s00222-023-01220-6 checked for updates, 22 September 2026: none registered; arXiv 2108.13397: v1 (30 August 2021), v2 (12 February 2022), v3 (15 September 2023, the latest); web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found; F. ### Burungale–Kobayashi–Ota, "Rubin's conjecture on local units in the anticyclotomic tower at inert primes", Annals of Mathematics 194 (2021), no. 3 (`PAPER-BURUNGALE-KOBAYASHI-OTA-21`)

- **Error** at Lemma 5.2(ii), p.956, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 194 (2021), 943–966 (Caltech repository copy of the publisher PDF, SHA-256 77ff3290…790e) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"(ii) The number of supersingular points in X₀(N)(F_{p²}) that correspond to supersingular elliptic curves over F_{p²} with the Frobenius trace a_{p²} = 2p or −2p and the j-invariant different from 0 and 1728 is at least µ(⌊(p−1)/12⌋ − 3/2 + (−3/p) + ½(−1/p)). In particular, there is a supersingular point with a_{p²} = 2p or −2p in X₀(N)(F_{p²}) over X(1)(F_{p²})∖{0,1728} that is unramified for π if p ≡ 1 mod 12 or p ≥ 31."`; it should be `The "in particular" follows for p ≡ 1 mod 12 and for p ≥ 31 with p ≠ 47. At p = 47 the displayed lower bound is 0 and gives nothing. The existence of an unramified supersingular point at p = 47 comes instead from Lemma 5.2(iii), since 47 ≡ 2 mod 3.`. The conclusion needs the lower bound to exceed the number of ramification points, which (i) bounds by µ/6. At p = 47: ⌊46/12⌋ = 3, (−3/47) = −1 (47 ≡ 2 mod 3) and (−1/47) = −1 (47 ≡ 3 mod 4), so the bound is µ(3 − 3/2 − 1 − 1/2) = 0 < µ/6. A computation over all primes 31 ≤ p ≤ 400 finds 47 as the only failure, and beyond that the floor term dominates. The main theorem is unaffected: Lemma 5.2(iii) covers p = 47, and Rubin's own verification (Remark 2.3) covers small p. Found by the review REV-PAPER-BURUNGALE-KOBAYASHI-OTA-21. Recorded as `PAPER-BURUNGALE-KOBAYASHI-OTA-21/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2021/194-3/p08, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or corrigendum is linked; arXiv: no version of this paper was found by arXiv and web searches; the later p = 3 extension (arXiv 2401.09037v2, 2 January 2025) was read and mentions no correction; web search for an erratum, corrigendum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found; authors' pages: A. A. Burungale's homepage lists no papers or corrections; the Kyushu University research portal entry for the paper shows no correction.
- **Misprint** at §3.0.2 and Proposition 3.3, pp.951–953; proof of Theorem 3.4, p.954, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 194 (2021), 943–966 (Caltech repository copy of the publisher PDF, SHA-256 77ff3290…790e). The source says `"Let ν ∈ X_l be a Hecke character of order l^m. We define ξ_ν as the image of z_{fl^m} …" and "Proposition 3.3. Let ν ∈ X_l be a character of order l^m", while the same construction uses K(fl^m p^{n+1}), "the conductor fl^m of ϕν" (proof of 3.3(3)) and "let l^m be the conductor" (p.954).`; it should be `Read l^m as the conductor of ν throughout §3.0.2–3.0.3 (so that ν factors through K(fl^m) and the elliptic unit z_{fl^m} applies), with the anticyclotomic layer L_m indexed accordingly.`. For l split in K, the l-part of the ring class group of conductor l^n is (Z/l^n)^×, of order (l−1)l^{n−1}, so an anticyclotomic character of exact order l^m (m ≥ 1) has conductor l^{m+1}, not l^m. The construction and the proofs use l^m as the conductor, so "order" is the slip; nothing else changes. The extraction silently used the conductor reading. - **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 5.5(ii), p.959, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 194 (2021), 943–966 (Caltech repository copy of the publisher PDF, SHA-256 77ff3290…790e). The source says `"The first type is permuted by the action of Gal(Ψ′_{s+1}/Ψ′_s). So each lattice of this type is of the form σx_s with σ ∈ Gal(Ψ′_{s+1}/Ψ′_s)."`; it should be `"of the form σx_{s+1}".`. The lattices of the first type are the index-p superlattices of forward conductor s+1, which correspond to x_{s+1}; the relation derived next, T_p x_s = Σ_σ σx_{s+1} + x_{s−1}, uses x_{s+1}. ### Burungale–Tian, "A rank zero p-converse to a theorem of Gross–Zagier, Kolyvagin and Rubin", Annals of Mathematics 203 (2026), no. 1 (`PAPER-BURUNGALE-TIAN-26`)

- **Error** at Theorem 2.1 and footnote 3, p.4, in arXiv 2506.03465v2 (11 October 2025), the version read; the passage is the same in v1 (4 June 2025) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Theorem 2.1. Let the notation be as above. Then we have ξ(h²_Q) = ξ((h¹/z)_Q), an equality of ideals in Z_p[[G_{p∞f}]]⊗Q." Footnote 3: "The formulation in Theorem 2.1 is then identical with the one in [11, §7.2]."`; it should be `ξ((h¹/z)_Q) = ξ(h²_Q)·∏_{𝔩|f, 𝔩∤p} ξ(Z_p[[G_{p∞f}/D_𝔩]]_Q), D_𝔩 the decomposition group of 𝔩; equivalently ξ((h¹/z)_Q) = ξ(H²_Q) with H² = lim_{K'} H²(O_{K'}[1/pf], Z_p(1)), which is the module of Johnson-Leung–Kings §7.2. The printed equality holds when every prime factor of f lies above p, and after localizing at height-one primes on components ramified at every 𝔩|f with 𝔩∤p.`. z is generated by the elliptic units of the single modulus p^n f (Kato §15.5), whose projection to a character χ carries ∏_{𝔩|f,𝔩∤p}(1 − χ(𝔩)Frob_𝔩^{−1}) (Johnson-Leung–Kings Lemma 7.7); JLK accordingly pair it with H² over O_K[1/pf]. BT's h² is the class-group limit, which Kato (15.6.2) identifies at height one with H² over O_K[1/p]. By purity, 0 → H²(O[1/p]) → H²(O[1/pf]) → ⊕_{𝔩|f,𝔩∤p} Z_p[[G/D_𝔩]] → 0, and since D_𝔩 has Z_p-rank one each term is torsion but not pseudo-null. On the trivial-character component 1 − Frob_𝔩^{−1} is a non-unit even after inverting p, so the printed equality fails whenever f has a prime factor not above p, and footnote 3's "identical" is false for h². The same imprecision is in Kato's quotation of Rubin (Astérisque 295, Theorem 15.2(2)); Rubin's elliptic units include all moduli g|f. ### Calegari–Dimitrov–Tang, "The unbounded denominators conjecture", Journal of the American Mathematical Society (2025) (`PAPER-CALEGARI-DIMITROV-TANG-25`)

- **Misprint** at Lemma 4.5.11, second line, p.663 (published version; the same in arXiv v3–v4). The source says `“dim H¹(Γ(5), F₃)^{SL₂(F₅)} = 0,” and, for N = 3, 4, 5, “the same result holds even after considering the semisimplifications of these modules.”`; it should be `dim H¹(Γ(5), 𝔽₅)^{SL₂(𝔽₅)} = 0 (coefficients 𝔽₅), and likewise for its semisimplification.`. Diagram (4.5.10) requires ℓ | N, and the proof of Theorem 4.5.2 takes N = ℓ ∈ {3, 5}, so ℓ = 5 is the case used. As printed the statement is false: SL₂(𝔽₅) is perfect, so inflation–restriction for Γ(5) ⊴ SL₂(ℤ) makes restriction H¹(SL₂(ℤ), 𝔽₃) = Hom(ℤ/12, 𝔽₃) ≅ 𝔽₃ → H¹(Γ(5), 𝔽₃)^{SL₂(𝔽₅)} injective (the character SL₂(ℤ) → SL₂(𝔽₃) → ℤ/3 stays nontrivial on Γ(5), which maps onto SL₂(𝔽₃)). Over 𝔽₃ the semisimplification claim also fails: H¹(Γ(5), ℚ) = 3 + 3′ + 5 as an A₅-module, and the 5-dimensional character, with Brauer character (5, 1, 0, 0) on the 3-regular classes, reduces mod 3 to 1 + 4. Over 𝔽₅ the lemma holds: H¹(SL₂(ℤ), 𝔽₅) = 0 and H²(SL₂(𝔽₅), 𝔽₅) = 0 (Lemma 4.5.5) give vanishing invariants, and 3, 3′, 5 reduce to the Brauer irreducibles 3, 3, 5 of PSL₂(𝔽₅), none trivial. - **Misprint** at §7.4.2, the relation between E and K, p.693 (published version; the same since arXiv v2). The source says `“(2/π)E(16x) = (16x − 1)(2/π)K(16x) − 8∫₀ˣ(2/π)K(16t) dt”`; it should be `(2/π)E(16x) = (1 − 16x)(2/π)K(16x) + 8∫₀ˣ(2/π)K(16t) dt.`. At x = 0 the printed right side is −1 and the left side is 1. With (2/π)K(16x) = Σ C(2n,n)²xⁿ and (2/π)E(16x) = ₂F₁(½, −½; 1; 16x), the printed right side equals −(2/π)E(16x) coefficientwise; this was checked in exact arithmetic through x³⁹ and numerically at x = 0.03. The integrality statements that follow concern ∫K and are correct. The printed ψ(k) − ψ(k+a) is increasing in a, so its maximum would be at a = 3/4. Only a uniform bound is needed. - **Error** at §4.4, the sentence before Lemma 4.4.4, p.659, and its use in (4.4.9)–(4.4.10), p.660 (published version; the same since arXiv v3) (it affects the proof). The source says `“There is a natural surjection π : ⟨E, Γ(N)⟩ ∩ Γ0(p) → B whose kernel is Γ(Np).”`; it should be `For N > 2 the kernel of reduction mod p on ⟨E, Γ(N)⟩ ∩ Γ₀(p) is ⟨E, Γ(N)⟩ ∩ Γ(p), which contains Γ(Np) with index 2. Restrict to Γ(N) ∩ Γ₀(p), where the kernel is Γ(Np): Lemma 4.4.4 holds there, and it gives ⟨G ∩ Γ(Np), A⁻¹GA ∩ Γ₀(p) ∩ Γ(N)⟩ = Γ(N) ∩ Γ₀(p); adjoining E gives (4.4.9) as printed. Twisting π by the sign x ≡ ±I mod N works equally.`. Some x ∈ SL₂(ℤ) has x ≡ −I mod N and x ≡ I mod p (SL₂(ℤ) → SL₂(ℤ/Np) is onto). It lies in ⟨E, Γ(N)⟩ ∩ Γ₀(p) and in ker π, but not in Γ(Np). So, as written, ker(f₁, π) = G ∩ Γ(p), and the deduction yields (4.4.9) only with G ∩ Γ(p). That is weaker than the (4.4.10) used in the proof of Theorem 4.3.1, where f(pτ) is known to be invariant only under G ∩ Γ(2Np). The proof of Lemma 4.4.4 already works on Γ(N) ∩ Γ₀(p): (f₁, f₂) stays surjective there, and π(γ) still generates the unipotent radical. So the corrected deduction goes through, and Theorems 4.3.1–4.3.2 stand. The source says `“The intersection of all these groups still has finite index and level 2N by Lemma 4.1.3, and then we take G2N to be the largest normal subgroup of SL2(Z) contained in this intersection, which also has L(G2N) = 2N by Lemma 4.1.4.”`; it should be `Intersect also with ⟨E, Γ(2N)⟩ before taking the normal core. The basis elements span R_{2N} over ℚ(λ), and λ is only Γ(2)-invariant, so nothing forces the intersection into ⟨E, Γ(2N)⟩, as the statement requires. For example, if R₂ = ℚ(λ) the basis {1} has stabilizer SL₂(ℤ). - **Misprint** at Proof of Lemma 5.2.18, p.678 (published version; the same in arXiv v4). The source says `“By Lemma 5.2.6, the set {z ∈ D(0, 1) : |FN(z)| > e^M + 1} is contained in S(M, N)”`; it should be `… is contained in s·S(M, N), the image of S(M, N) under the order-2N rotation s of Lemma 5.2.6.`. On that set |1 − F_N^N| ≥ |F_N|^N − 1 > e^{MN}, so it is disjoint from S(M, N). By (5.2.7) it lies in s·S(M, N). Since s is a Euclidean rotation of D(0,1) about 0, s·S(M, N) also avoids the closed disc of radius r, and the conclusion stands. arXiv v1 (“contained in the rotation under s of the set S(M, N)”) and v3 (“contained in sS(M, N)”) had it right; the s was lost in v4. In the same proof the Shimizu bound is quoted for γ̃ ∈ Γ̃_N but used for γ ∈ Φ_N; Φ̃_N is discrete and contains t̃_N, so Shimizu's lemma gives the same bound. With s_1 ↦ z and s_2 ↦ 0, both forms pull back to dz, which is nonzero in (k[z]/(z²))dz, so φ is not ω_1- or ω_2-integral and the example does not show sharpness. The source says `"If Z_1 and Z_2 are algebraic cycles of dimensions r_1 and r_2, the intersection Z_1.Z_2 is an algebraic cycle of dimension 2n − r_1 − r_2 defined up to algebraic equivalence."`; it should be `Of dimension r_1 + r_2 − n.`. On an n-dimensional abelian variety, cycles of dimensions r_1 and r_2 have codimensions n − r_1 and n − r_2. Their intersection has codimension 2n − r_1 − r_2, that is, dimension r_1 + r_2 − n. The later computations, for example deg(H^{n−2}.3H.X) on p.20, use the correct count. Noted by the extraction. - **Misprint** at Proof of Lemma 5.4, p.17, in arXiv 2102.01055v2 (10 February 2021, the latest arXiv version, 39 pp.; PDF SHA-256 d7398c4b…67f7); the published article, Invent. Math. 234 (2023), 1197–1250 (54 pp., substantially revised), was not accessible and was not collated, so this finding concerns v2. The source says `"… where the last equality uses tr(α) = −2(γ_1 + ... + γ_n) and (5.1)."`; it should be `tr(α) = 2(γ_1 + … + γ_n).`. P_α(t) = Q(t)² with Q monic of degree n and roots γ_i, so the coefficient of t^{2n−1} in P_α is −2Σγ_i. The paper defines tr(ψ) as the negative of that coefficient (§5.1), so tr α = 2Σγ_i. Combined with (5.1), tr α_A(C,H) = 2 deg(C.H), this gives Σγ_i = deg(C.H), which is what the arithmetic–geometric mean step uses. With the printed sign, the positive γ_i would have negative sum. Noted by the extraction (gap G3). - **Error** at Proof of Lemma 5.5(i), p.18, in arXiv 2102.01055v2 (10 February 2021, the latest arXiv version, 39 pp.; PDF SHA-256 d7398c4b…67f7); the published article, Invent. Math. 234 (2023), 1197–1250 (54 pp., substantially revised), was not accessible and was not collated, so this finding concerns v2 (it affects the proof). The source says `"If (i) holds we consider the ample divisor H = X and note that Lemma 5.3 gives deg_H(X) = deg(X³)_A = cdeg(X) = c_1²(X). Thus, condition (5.4) in case (i) simplifies to p > 2c_1²(X)/3, which is implied by (5.3)." (Here (5.3) is p > c_1²(X).)`; it should be `With n = 3 and H = X, condition (5.4) reads p > (3!/3³) · c_1²(X)³ / c_1²(X) = 2c_1²(X)²/9. Lemma 5.5(i) should assume p > max(c_1²(X), 2c_1²(X)²/9). The hypothesis p > (128/9)c_1²(X)² of Theorem 9.1(i), under which the lemma is used (Lemma 9.10), implies this.`. The substitution gives 2s²/9 with s = c_1²(X), not 2s/3. For s ≥ 5, (5.3) does not imply it; for example s = 6 and p = 7 satisfy p > s but not p > 2s²/9 = 8. So the proof of case (a) under (i), which applies Lemma 5.4, is not justified by (5.3) alone. The lemma may still hold at the printed threshold, but the argument does not show it. The main theorems assume the stronger bound. Checked on the page image. - **Error** at §7.1–7.2, the series Ψ^{[m]}, Lemma 7.4 and the definitions of Log^t and Exp^t, pp.23–24, in arXiv 2102.01055v2 (10 February 2021, the latest arXiv version, 39 pp.; PDF SHA-256 d7398c4b…67f7); the published article, Invent. Math. 234 (2023), 1197–1250 (54 pp., substantially revised), was not accessible and was not collated, so this finding concerns v2 (it affects the proof). The source says `"Given an integer m, let Ψ^{[m]} = (Ψ^{[m]}_1, ..., Ψ^{[m]}_n) be the power series expansion of the morphism [m] : A → A of multiplication by m in terms of t." "From [11] III.5.3 Proposition 2(i) we get Lemma 7.4. The terms of degree less than m in Ψ^{[m]}_j vanish for each j = 1, ..., n." "Log^t_j(y) = Σ_{m≥1} ((−1)^m/m) · Ψ^{[m]}_j(y), Exp^t_j(x) = Σ_{m≥1} (1/m!) · ψ^{[m]}_j(x)", with ψ^{[m]}_j the homogeneous part of degree m of Ψ^{[m]}_j.`; it should be `Take Ψ^{[m]} to be the m-th finite difference of the multiplication maps, Ψ^{[m]} = Σ_{i=0}^{m} (−1)^{m−i} C(m,i) [i], the series of Bourbaki III.5.3. Take Log^t_j(y) = Σ_{m≥1} ((−1)^{m−1}/m) Ψ^{[m]}_j(y). With these changes Lemma 7.4 is Bourbaki's statement. Lemma 7.2 still holds, as Ψ^{[m]} is an integral combination. Exp^t is the exponential, and the proofs of Lemmas 7.5–7.7 go through as written.`. As defined, Ψ^{[m]} is multiplication by m, whose linear term is m·t_j, so Lemma 7.4 fails already for the additive formal group and m = 2. Then Log^t_j has linear term Σ_m (−1)^m y_j, which does not converge coefficientwise. The printed Exp^t is also wrong in general. For the one-dimensional formal group with logarithm f(t) = t + at² + bt³, the printed Exp^t (degree-m parts of [m], weighted by 1/m!) has cubic coefficient 6a² − 4b, while the true exponential f^{−1} has 2a² − b; the finite differences give the correct 2a² − b, checked by exact computation to degree 4. The sign: with finite differences, Σ ((−1)^{m−1}/m) Ψ^{[m]} equals the logarithm f, while the printed (−1)^m/m gives −f, which is not inverse to Exp^t as Lemma 7.7(ii) asserts. The bounds used later (|b_{j,α}| ≤ ‖α‖^{[K:Q_p]}, m!c_{j,α} ∈ R) hold for the corrected series, so the main results are unaffected. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (gap G4, items formal-low-degree-source, formal-log-source and formal-exp), which proposed a different repair; the finite-difference reading is given here. The step from b_1v_1 + b_2v_2 ∈ ϖR^n to b_1v_1 + b_2v_2 ∈ ϖ(Rv_1 + Rv_2) assumes that Rv_1 + Rv_2 is saturated in R^n. ### Česnavičius, "Purity for the Brauer group", Duke Mathematical Journal (2019) (`PAPER-CESNAVICIUS-19`)

- **Gap** at Proposition 2.2 and its proof, p.4, in arXiv 1711.06456v4 (1 December 2018, the latest version; PDF SHA-256 a62a12bb…8709); the author's copy of 27 November 2018 on his homepage has the same text; the published text, Duke Math. J. 168 (2019), 1461–1486, was not collated (it affects the proof). The source says `"Proposition 2.2. For a finite, flat map R → R′ of local rings, an open subscheme V ⊂ Spec R, and an affine, smooth R-group scheme G, if (1) Γ(Spec R, O) ≅ Γ(V, O) via pullback; and (2) every G-torsor is trivial over R; then the following pullback is injective: H¹_et(V, G) ↪ H¹_et(V_{R′}, G). (2.2.1)" Proof: "… every element of X(V) lifts to (Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′}))(V), so, by [Gir71, III.3.2.2], the map H¹_et(V, G) → H¹_et(V, Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′})) (2.2.2) is injective."`; it should be `State Proposition 2.2 for commutative G, which covers both of its uses (Proposition 2.3, with G = Res_{R′/R}(T_{R′})/T, and Corollary 2.4, with G = T). For noncommutative G, supply the missing step: for every G-torsor P over V, the twisted map (^P Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′}))(V) → (^P X)(V) must also be surjective.`. Write H = Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′}) and X = H/G. Surjectivity of H(V) → X(V) shows that the fibre of H¹(V, G) → H¹(V, H) over the trivial class is trivial. When G is commutative this is injectivity, because the map is a group homomorphism. For a noncommutative G, H¹ is only a pointed set. The fibre through the class of a torsor P is the orbit set of (^P H)(V) acting on (^P X)(V), for the twists by P (Giraud III.3.2; cf. Serre, Galois Cohomology, I.5.5, Cor. 2). The proof checks only the untwisted case. The twisted forms ^P X live only over V, so hypothesis (1) and the affineness of X give no control of their V-points. If P extends to R it is trivial by (2), so the problem is exactly the torsors on V that do not extend. Proposition 2.2 is used only in the proofs of Proposition 2.3 (G = Res_{R′/R}(T_{R′})/T) and Corollary 2.4 (G = T, an R-torus), where G is commutative, so the main theorems are unaffected. Noted by the extraction (gap G-NONABELIAN, items h1-descent and h1-abelian), which did not claim the statement false; neither is it claimed false here. Recorded as `PAPER-CESNAVICIUS-19/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Duke Mathematical Journal: Crossref record of DOI 10.1215/00127094-2018-0057, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or other update is registered; arXiv 1711.06456: v1 (17 November 2017), v2 (24 November 2017), v3 (17 January 2018), v4 (1 December 2018, the latest); the author's copy https://www.imo.universite-paris-saclay.fr/~kestutis.cesnavicius/brauer-purity.pdf (dated 27 November 2018, SHA-256 4187331e…97a1), checked 22 September 2026: Proposition 2.2 and its proof are unchanged, and no erratum is linked; web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.

### François Charles, "Birational boundedness for holomorphic symplectic varieties, Zarhin's trick for K3 surfaces, and the Tate conjecture", Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), no. 2 (`PAPER-CHARLES-16`)

- **Error** at Corollary 2.8 and its proof, pp.499–500, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 487–526 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3425f269…40e2) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"If p > 0, we can find nonnegative integers λ and t such that 0 < λ ≤ v² and |disc(NS(X))| = p^t λ |disc(NS(M_H(v)))|." Proof: "… we have a natural injection of lattices with torsion cokernel Zv ⊕ (v^⊥ ∩ N(X)) ↪ N(X). Since the discriminant of Zv ⊕ v^⊥ is v² disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X)), this implies by the same argument that |disc(N(X))| ≤ v² |disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X))|."`; it should be `λ need not be an integer. With I = [N(X) : Zv ⊕ (v^⊥ ∩ N(X))], one has |disc NS(X)| = p^t (v²/I²) |disc NS(M_H(v))| with t ≥ 0. So λ = v²/I² is a positive rational number with λ ≤ v², and I divides v² because v is primitive. For p ∤ v², for example v² = 2 and p ≥ 3 as in the applications, λ is still a p-adic unit, which is what the later arguments use.`. Take v = (1, 0, −1), the Mukai vector of ideal sheaves of length-two subschemes, so M_H(v) = X^[2]. It satisfies condition (C): it is primitive, of rank 1, v² = 2, and gcd(1, 0, −1) = 1. Then v^⊥ ∩ N(X) = NS(X) ⊕ Z(1, 0, 1) with (1, 0, 1)² = −2, so |disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X))| = 2|disc NS(X)|. The proof's first step gives |disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X))| = p^r |disc NS(M_H(v))|. Hence |disc NS(X)| = ½ p^r |disc NS(M_H(v))|, which is not of the form p^t λ with λ a positive integer when p is odd. The proof passes from the equality disc(Zv ⊕ K) = I² disc N(X) to an integer multiplier without controlling I, here I = 2. The inequality stated for p = 0 is correct. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (items 34–35). Charles's publication page https://www.math.ens.psl.eu/~charles/, checked 22 September 2026: it links an erratum for Invent. Math. 194 (2013) only, not for this paper; web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Error** at Proposition 2.9, p.500, as used in the proof of Theorem 2.10, p.503, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 487–526 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3425f269…40e2) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Proposition 2.9. Let d be a positive integer, and let Λ be a rank 2 positive definite sublattice of Λ_2d. There exists a positive integer N and nonzero integers a, b such that if m is any positive integer satisfying (i) m = 1[N]; (ii) m is prime to a and b, and both a and b are quadratic residues modulo m; then there exists a primitive embedding of Λ into Λ_2md." Proof: "In the setting of the proposition, the primitive embedding of Λ into Λ_2d corresponds to a tuple (V, W, γ, t)."`; it should be `Assume Λ is a primitive sublattice of Λ_2d. In the proof of Theorem 2.10, choose the lattice Λ containing v, w with v² = 2, v·w = 1 to be primitive. For example, in Λ_2d = ⟨2d⟩ ⊕ U with basis h, e, f, take the span of v = e + f and w = kh + e. It is primitive, since (x, y, z) ↦ (z, y − z) retracts it, and its Gram matrix [[2, 1], [1, 2dk²]] is positive definite.`. The proof starts from a primitive embedding Λ ⊂ Λ_2d, which the statement does not provide, and the statement fails for non-primitive Λ. In Λ_2 = ⟨2⟩ ⊕ U, the sublattice Λ generated by 2h and 2(e + f) has Gram matrix diag(8, 8). Take m = 1, which satisfies (i) and (ii) for every N, a, b. A primitive embedding of Λ into Λ_2 would give a 2-dimensional subspace of Λ_2/2Λ_2 = F_2³ on which the half-norm Q(x, y, z) = x² + yz vanishes, since Λ has all half-norms even. The nonzero zeros of Q are (0,0,1), (0,1,0) and (1,1,1), and each pairwise sum has Q = 1, so no such plane exists. Noted by the extraction (items 38–41), which also gave the primitive choice above. - **Gap** at Proof of Proposition 3.1, p.506, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 487–526 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3425f269…40e2) (it affects the proof). The source says `"By Riemann-Roch, for any p and q as above, the morphism H⁰(C, 3L|_C) → 3L|_C/(3L|_C − D(p) − D(q)) is surjective. Indeed, the degree of 3L|_C − D(p) − D(q) is 3r − 4 > r = deg(ω_{C/k}); note that r is even, so that r > 1."`; it should be `Pass to 2L before this step, as the proof already allows ("up to replacing L by 2L"), so that r = L² ≥ 4 and 3r − 4 > r holds.`. 3r − 4 > r holds only for r > 2. "r is even, so that r > 1" leaves r = 2, the double-plane case, where 3r − 4 = r and the surjectivity argument as written does not apply. The proposition only claims the existence of some L′ with bounded invariants, so doubling L is allowed. Noted by the extraction (item 50), which also leaves the characteristic-two nonreduced-curve argument as an open check. - **Error** at Proposition 3.17, p.517, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 487–526 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3425f269…40e2) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Then there exist a positive integer N and nonzero integers a, b such that there exist only finitely many polarized nonsuperspecial K3 surfaces (X, H) of degree 2md over k, where m is a positive integer satisfying (i) m = 1[N]; (ii) m is prime to a and b, and both a and b are quadratic residues modulo m; (iii) the p-adic valuation of the discriminant of NS(X_k̄) is at most t_0."`; it should be `There are only finitely many k-isomorphism classes of nonsuperspecial K3 surfaces X over k admitting a polarization H of such a degree 2md with (i)–(iii). The polarized pairs themselves need not be finite in number.`. On a fixed eligible (X, H) of degree 2m_0 d, the line bundles H^{⊗ℓ^j} have degree 2(ℓ^{2j} m_0)d. Choose a prime ℓ with a and b quadratic residues modulo ℓ and prime to N, a and b, and let j run through multiples of the order of ℓ² modulo N. Then m = ℓ^{2j} m_0 satisfies (i) and (ii), and (iii) is unchanged. This gives infinitely many pairwise non-isomorphic polarized pairs on one surface; the paper does not require polarizations to be primitive. The proof bounds NS(X_k̄) and then the surfaces. The only use, the proof of Theorem 1.3 on p.523, reads "there exist only finitely many K3 surfaces over k", so the corrected statement is what is used. Noted by the extraction (items 88–89). Recorded as `PAPER-CHARLES-16/E5`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2016/184-2/p04 and the Crossref record of DOI 10.4007/annals.2016.184.2.4, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or update; arXiv 1407.0592: v1 (2 July 2014) and v2 (24 August 2014, the latest); F. - **Error** at §4.1, the definition of N_{α/r}(X) and (4.1), pp.518–519, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 487–526 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3425f269…40e2). The source says `"Let N_{α/r}(X) be the preimage of N(X) by T_{−α/r}. This is the group denoted by CH_{α/r}(X, Z) in [LMS14]. By [LMS14, Lemma 3.3.3], we have (4.1) N_{α/r}(X) = {(ar, D + aα, cω) | a, c ∈ Z, D ∈ NS(X)}."`; it should be `Define N_{α/r}(X) directly as the lattice (4.1), the twisted Mukai lattice CH_{α/r}(X, Z) of [LMS14], and drop "the preimage of N(X) by T_{−α/r}".`. With T_{−α/r}(x) = x ∪ e^{−α/r} and e^{−α/r} = (1, −α/r, α²/(2r²)), one computes T_{−α/r}(ar, D + aα, cω) = (ar, D, (c − (α·D)/r − aα²/(2r))ω). Its last coordinate is not an integer in general, for α ∈ H²(X, Z_ℓ(1)) primitive and r = ℓ^n. So the lattice (4.1) is not the preimage of N(X). Lemma 4.2, Proposition 4.3 and §4.2 compute with the explicit lattice (4.1), so the results are unaffected. Noted by the extraction (§4.1 conventions, items 93–106). ### William Y. Chen, "Nonabelian level structures, Nielsen equivalence, and Markoff triples", Annals of Mathematics 199 (2024), no. 1 (`PAPER-CHEN-24`)

- **Error** at §5.6, p. 85, Proposition 5.6.1(a) (arXiv v2). The source says `There is a unique unramified point in f^{−1}(0). Every other point is ramified with index 3, so |f^{−1}(0)| = (p(p+3)−3)/3 if p ≡ 1 mod 4, (p(p−3)−3)/3 if p ≡ 3 mod 4`; it should be `|f^{−1}(0)| = 1 + (deg f − 1)/3 = (p(p+3)+2)/3 for p ≡ 1 mod 4 and (p(p−3)+2)/3 for p ≡ 3 mod 4.`. The printed expression is never an integer: deg f = |X*(p)| = p(p±3) ≡ p² ≡ 1 (mod 3) for every prime p ≠ 3, so 3 never divides deg f − 3. The sentence preceding the display gives the correct count, one unramified point together with (deg f − 1)/3 points of ramification index 3. Computing the γ₀-orbits on X*(p) directly confirms it: for p = 5, 7, 11, 13, 17, 19, 23 the fibre has 14, 10, 30, 70, 114, 102, 154 points and γ₀ has exactly one fixed point in each case, matching (deg f + 2)/3 = 14, 10, 30, 70, 114, 102, 154 and not (deg f − 3)/3 = 37/3, 25/3, 85/3, 205/3, 337/3, 301/3, 457/3. Recorded as `PAPER-CHEN-24/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing of 2011.12940 on 2026-09-22: two versions (25 November 2020 and 19 June 2021) and the author's comment, which announces a revision but no erratum; Crossref for 10.4007/annals.2024.199.1.5 and for works correcting it on 2026-09-22: no corrigendum; the Annals of Mathematics listing for volume 199 (2024), no. 1 on 2026-09-22: no erratum linked.
- **Misprint** at §5.6, p. 85, Proposition 5.6.1(b), first sentence (arXiv v2). The source says `There are precisely two unramified points in π^{−1}(1728) if p ≡ 1, 7 mod 8, and no unramified points otherwise.`; it should be `f^{−1}(1728), the fibre of the forgetful map f : M_p → M(1) named in the statement; π is not defined in this proposition.`. The rest of the proposition, and part (a), use f for the map M_p → M(1); π is used elsewhere in the paper for the universal admissible cover C → E. The displayed formula in (b) is for |f^{−1}(1728)| and is correct: computing the γ₁₇₂₈-orbits on X*(p) gives 20, 15, 44, 104, 171, 152, 231 for p = 5, 7, 11, 13, 17, 19, 23, with 0, 2, 0, 0, 2, 0, 2 fixed points, matching the four cases of the display and the claim about p ≡ 1, 7 mod 8. Recorded as `PAPER-CHEN-24/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing of 2011.12940 on 2026-09-22: two versions (25 November 2020 and 19 June 2021) and the author's comment, which announces a revision but no erratum; Crossref for 10.4007/annals.2024.199.1.5 and for works correcting it on 2026-09-22: no corrigendum; the Annals of Mathematics listing for volume 199 (2024), no. 1 on 2026-09-22: no erratum linked.

(5) G^∨ = E_8: (a) O^∨ = D_6: … n_v(17) = n_u(17) = 0, n_v(16) = 2 > 1 = n_v(16)."`; it should be `n_v(9) = n_u(9) = 0 in (3)(a); n_v(9) = 1 > 0 = n_u(9) in (4); n_v(16) = 2 > 1 = n_u(16) in (5)(a).`. Kakde's homepage, p.52. The February 2022 version on S. Dasgupta's homepage (p.53) does not have it. The published version, Annals of Mathematics 197 (2023), 289–388, could not be checked (paywalled).. The source says `"For a cusp [𝒜] ∈ C₀(𝔠, 𝔫), the normalized constant term c_𝒜(0, W_k(ψ_𝔓, 1)) equals 𝔱 · τ(ψ)/N𝔠₀^k · sgn(N(−c)) ψ(𝔠_𝒜) · L(ψ^{−1}, 1−k)/2^n · ∏_{𝔭|𝔓}(1 − ψ(𝔭)/N𝔭^k) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱}(1 − ψ(𝔩)) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱^c}(1 − N𝔩^k)."`; it should be `Delete the leading 𝔱: c_𝒜(0, W_k(ψ_𝔓, 1)) = τ(ψ)/N𝔠₀^k · sgn(N(−c)) ψ(𝔠_𝒜) · L(ψ^{−1}, 1−k)/2^n · ∏_{𝔭|𝔓}(1 − ψ(𝔭)/N𝔭^k) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱}(1 − ψ(𝔩)) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱^c}(1 − N𝔩^k).`. 𝔱 is an integral ideal (the auxiliary modulus of §8.1), not a scalar, so the printed product has no meaning. The proof computes the constant term as the displayed expression with Σ_{𝔪|𝔱} μ(𝔪) ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔪^c} N𝔩^k ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔪} ψ(𝔩) in place of the last two products, and evaluates that sum by the Möbius identity; no factor 𝔱 appears. The February 2022 version prints the formula without it (with 𝔮 for 𝔩). Checked on the page images of both author versions and in the arXiv v3 source (`\ft` at the start of the display). Noted by the extraction (item 221). - **Error** at Proof of Theorem 7.1, p.41 (choice of η_v at finite places) (arXiv v2; published Annals version not collated) (it affects the proof). The source says `"At each non-archimedean place v of K, we set η_v := min{1, |t₁(t₁ − 1)|_v, |t₂(t₂ − 1)|_v}. … (µ_{t_i,v}, m_{F,η,v})_v = (µ_{t_i,v}, [F]_v)_v for this choice of η, because the potentials for µ_{t_i,v} are constant on disks of radius η_v."`; it should be `At places v | 2 take η_v := |2|_v · min{1, |t₁(t₁ − 1)|_v, |t₂(t₂ − 1)|_v}. The claim is then true: in the potentially good range the support of µ_{t,v} is a type II point of diameter |Δ_t|_v^{1/6} = |2|_v^{2/3}|t(t − 1)|_v^{1/3} ≥ |2|_v min{1, |t(t − 1)|_v}, and in the bad-reduction ranges of Proposition 3.5 smaller radii are harmless. (7.5) grows by Σ_{v|2} r_v log 2 = log 2, which is divided by 2|F| and absorbed into C(ε).`. The claim fails at v | 2 when |t_i(t_i − 1)|_v = 1. There η_v = 1, but µ_{t_i,v} is a point mass at ζ_{a,R} with R < 1. Indeed λ_{t,v}(0) = λ_{t,v}(ζ_{0,1}) = 0 and f_t(ζ_{0,1}) = ζ_{0,1/|4|}, so the support lies in a residue class of ζ_{0,1} other than that of 0. For x ∈ D(a,R), λ(ζ_{x,1}) − λ(x) = log(1/R) > 0, so the non-archimedean term of Proposition 7.3 is positive, not 0. Counterexample: take ω a primitive cube root of 1 and t₁ ≠ t₂ the roots of t² − (6ω² − 4)t − (3ω − 4) = 0 (discriminant 48ω(1 − ω) ≠ 0). Then ψ₃(ω, t_i) = 0, so x = ω ∈ π(E_{t₁}[3]) ∩ π(E_{t₂}[3]) ⊆ F. Also t₁t₂ = 4 − 3ω and (t₁ − 1)(t₂ − 1) = 15 + 3ω are 2-adic units, so |t_i(t_i − 1)|_v = 1 and η_v = 1. The reduction is supersingular (|j(t_i)|_v ≤ |2|^8), so the 3-torsion reduces to non-identity points and x ∈ D(a_i, R_i). The bound therefore acquires a positive term that is not divided by |F|, which would break the small-height case of Theorem 8.1. With the corrected η_v the equality holds and all later statements stand. Recorded as `PAPER-DEMARCO-KRIEGER-YE-20/E7`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 1901.09945 versions (v2, 3 December 2019, the post-referee version, is the latest); Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 949–1001 article page: no erratum listed; the typeset PDF was not accessible here, so the published text was not collated; Crossref metadata for 10.4007/annals.2020.191.3.5: no update or correction relation.
- **Gap** at Proof of Theorem 1.9, §9.4, p.47 (arXiv v2; published Annals version not collated) (it affects the proof). The source says `"Restricting to the points x ∈ j_P(X)(Q̄), so that π(Φ(x)₁) = π(Φ(x)₂) in ℙ¹, the theorem now follows from Theorem 8.1."`; it should be `The identity π(Φ(x)₁) = π(Φ(x)₂) holds on j_Q(X) for the Weierstrass point Q of Proposition 9.1. For another Weierstrass point P, j_P(X) = j_Q(X) + T with T = [Q − P] ∈ J(X)[2]. Since L_X is symmetric, ĥ_{L_X} is a quadratic form whose bilinear pairing kills torsion, so ĥ_{L_X}(x + T) = ĥ_{L_X}(x), and the count for P equals the count for Q. This is the analogue of the step written out for Theorem 1.1 in §9.3.`. For P ≠ Q, Φ(j_P(X)) = Φ(j_Q(X)) + Φ(T) is a 2-torsion translate of the lift of the diagonal, and π(Φ(x)₁) ≠ π(Φ(x)₂) in general, so the displayed restriction does not apply as written. Recorded as `PAPER-DEMARCO-KRIEGER-YE-20/E8`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 1901.09945 versions (v2, 3 December 2019, the post-referee version, is the latest); Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 949–1001 article page: no erratum listed; the typeset PDF was not accessible here, so the published text was not collated; Crossref metadata for 10.4007/annals.2020.191.3.5: no update or correction relation.

The source says `"… and to Σ_{λ,ε} := {f ∈ C_λ ∩ M_d(Q̄) : ĥ_crit(f) < ε/(ℓ+1)} ⊂ C_λ for λ ∈ U(ℂ) ∖ U(Q̄) … repeating this procedure provides uniform bounds on the Zariski closures of Σ^r_{λ,ε′} for all ε′ < ε and of X_λ ∩ PCF_d for all λ ∈ U(ℂ)."`; it should be `For λ ∈ V(ℂ) ∖ V(Q̄), X_λ ∩ PCF_d can contain flexible Lattès maps not defined over Q̄. These do not lie in Σ_{λ,ε}, whose points are in M_d(Q̄). Since X_λ ∩ PCF_d ∖ M_d(Q̄) ⊂ L_d (Thurston rigidity, §2.2), add the components of X_λ ∩ L_d. By Bézout they number at most D·deg L_d plus the number of components of L_d, with degrees ≤ max{deg L_d, 1}, uniformly in λ. The theorems are unaffected; only N and B change.`. The count in §5 bounds only algebraic points of small critical height, but Theorem 1.1 is stated for arbitrary complex X and all PCF points. Flexible Lattès maps are PCF and form a curve L_d ⊂ M_d in square degrees whose points with transcendental j-invariant are not defined over Q̄. So for d a square and transcendental λ, a curve C_λ can meet L_d in PCF points outside Σ_{λ,ε}. L = ⊗_c ⟨L_Φ|_{Γ_c}⟩ is a tensor product over all marked tuples c for f₁,…,f_ℓ, so its height counts each critical point of each f_i many times. Only the vanishing at PCF points and the comparison with Σ ĥ_crit are used, so nothing downstream changes. - **Gap** at Proof of Theorem 1.4, p.36 (arXiv v3; published Annals version not collated) (it affects the proof). The source says `"By the Northcott property and Torelli's Theorem, there are up-to Q̄-isomophism only finitely many C_Q̄'s … such that h(ι([Jac(C_Q̄)])) < c1. By applying Raynaud's result on the Manin–Mumford Conjecture to each one of these finitely many curves separately, we obtain Theorem 1.4."`; it should be `For each of the finitely many curves C a bound for #((C − P₀) ∩ Jac(C)_tors) uniform in P₀ ∈ C(ℚ̄) is needed, because Theorem 1.4 quantifies over all P₀. For g ≥ 3 it follows from Raynaud's theorem for the surface C − C ⊆ Jac(C): its torsion lies in finitely many points and elliptic torsion cosets, and a coset t + E pulls back to a curve in C × C with no component C × {P₀}, so each P₀ meets it in boundedly many points. For g = 2, C − C = Jac(C), and a uniform Manin–Mumford for the translates C − P₀ is required, e.g. Kühne's later uniform Manin–Mumford theorem (arXiv:2101.10272), which proves Theorem 1.4 without the dependence on d.`. Raynaud's theorem gives, for each embedding C − P₀ ⊆ Jac(C), a finite set, with no control of its size as P₀ varies over the infinitely many points of C(ℚ̄). The statement is true, but the displayed step does not prove it. The source says `"For an field F and a ∈ F^× set ⟨⟨a⟩⟩ = x_1² − a x_2², respectively ⟨⟨a]] := x_1² + x_1x_2 + a x_2². … If char(F) = 2, then q_a := q_{a_i,…,a_0} := ⟨⟨a_i⟩⟩ ⊗ … ⟨⟨a_1⟩⟩ ⊗ ⟨⟨a_0]]."`; it should be `In characteristic 2 the factors ⟨⟨a_j⟩⟩ (j ≥ 1) are the bilinear Pfister forms ⟨1, −a_j⟩_b of [EKM, 9.B], and q_a is their product with the quadratic form ⟨⟨a_0]]: a nonsingular quadratic form of dimension 2^{i+1}.`. With ⟨⟨a⟩⟩ read as the quadratic form x_1² − a x_2² just defined, the tensor product in characteristic 2 is not defined (quadratic forms do not multiply there), and that form is totally singular, being (x_1 + √a x_2)². Γ(s + 1/2 + r))."`; it should be `t^{1/2} J_{s−1/2}(t) = √2 Σ_{r≥0} (−1)^r (t/2)^{s+2r} / (r! Γ(s + 1/2 + r)).`. Put ν = s − 1/2 in J_ν(t) = Σ (−1)^r (t/2)^{ν+2r} / (r! Γ(ν + r + 1)). - **Gap** at DIT16 publisher Appendix A pp983–985, proof of(A.1); page images checked2026-09-22 (it affects the proof). The source says `“the Taylor series coefficients of both sides agree up to order 2”`; it should be `Use f=t^sH with H analytic (indeed entire), justify the endpoint integration and differentiated integrals, then derive c₁=0 and c_n=−c_{n−2}/[n(n+2s−1)]. This determines the solution, including s=1/2 and c₀=0.`. For nonintegral complex s the integral generally is not an ordinary Taylor series at0, and its ODE has a singular coefficient t⁻². A regular-point ODE uniqueness argument from two ordinary Taylor coefficients is therefore not the missing justification. Items162–167 and118 give the explicit entire-H argument, endpoint majorants and cutoff integration by parts. This is a proof-completion qualification, not a counterexample to Lemma7. - **Error** at Theorem 4, p.965, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 949–990 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 a67de715…5f61); the author preprint of 29 July 2016 on W. Duke's page has the same text there (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Then there exists a unique nonzero F(z) with weight 1/2 for Γ_0(4) with Fourier expansion F(z) = Σ b(n) W_{sgn(n)/4, ir/2}(4π|n|y) e(nx), such that for any pair of co-prime fundamental discriminants d′ and d, we have (5.16) 12√π |D|^{3/4} b(d′) \overline{b(d)} = ⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1} Σ_A χ(A) {…}. Here ⟨F, F⟩ = … = 1 and the value of b(n) for a general discriminant n = dm² … is determined by means of the Shimura relation …"`; it should be `F is unique only up to a constant factor of absolute value 1. The line spanned by F is unique, and F itself becomes unique once one also requires, for example, b(d_0) > 0 for one fundamental discriminant d_0 with b(d_0) ≠ 0.`. If F satisfies the conditions, so does uF for every complex u with |u| = 1. Indeed ⟨uF, uF⟩ = 1, the Shimura relation is linear in b, and b(d′)\overline{b(d)} is unchanged since |u|² = 1. Taking u = −1 gives a second such F. The conjugate in (5.16) was checked on the page image. Existence and the identity (5.16) are unaffected. The proof, via Proposition 6 in §10, produces F as a member ψ of a chosen orthonormal basis B_r, which fixes it only up to such a unit. Noted by the extraction (Corrections, item 5). - **Misprint** at §5, the numerical example after Theorem 4, p.966; also Proposition 6 and its proof, pp.981–982, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 949–990 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 a67de715…5f61); the author preprint of 29 July 2016 on W. Duke's page has the same text there. The source says `p.966: "we take the first occurring even Hecke-Maass form with eigenvalue λ = 190.13154731 ··· = 1/2 + r², where r/2 = 6.889875675 ···". Proposition 6, p.981: "For any even Hecke-Maass cusp form φ for Γ with Laplace eigenvalue 1/2 + r²". The same "1/2 + r²" appears again after (10.4) on p.981 and twice on p.982.`; it should be `1/4 + r² in each place.`. The paper writes every eigenvalue as λ = 1/4 + r² in (5.6), p.962, and Theorem 4 and Proposition 6 use the weight-1/2 Whittaker parameter ir/2 that belongs to 1/4 + r². The numbers confirm it. r = 2 × 6.889875675 = 13.77975135 gives 1/4 + r² = 190.131547…, the printed λ, whereas 1/2 + r² = 190.381547…. Noted by the extraction (Corrections, item 2).
